The Response of Optical Fibres to Gravitational Waves
Thomas B. Mieling

Abstract
The response of optical fibre modes to plane gravitational waves of low frequency is computed. By solving perturbatively the Maxwell equations for step-index optical fibres in a gravitational wave background and implementing appropriate boundary conditions to describe single-mode fibres, explicit formulae for the perturbations of the phase and the polarisation of the fibre modes are obtained.



\institution

University of Vienna, Faculty of Physics, Vienna, Austria,

TURIS Research Platform, University of Vienna, Austria

August 10, 2026

\smalltableofcontents

1
Introduction and Summary

While free space laser interferometric detectors of gravitational waves (
gw
’s) such as
ligo
and
virgo
are mainly analysed in the low-frequency regime
\omega_{g}\ll\omega
where the
gw
frequency is much smaller than the laser frequency (using either the geodesic deviation equation
[
1
,
2
,
3
]
, geometrical optics
[
4
,
5
,
6
,
7
,
8
,
9
]
or Maxwell’s equations directly
[
10
,
11
,
12
]
),
gw
detectors employing electromagnetic waveguides are typically discussed in the high-frequency regime
[
13
,
11
,
14
,
15
,
16
]
.
In this work, we bring together aspects of both of these kinds of analyses by studying an electromagnetic waveguide in the low-frequency regime.

One of our main motivations for such calculations is to study the effect of
gw
’s on light in regimes which are beyond a description in terms of geometrical optics. While such descriptions suffice to describe light propagation in vacuum or in dielectrics of large extensions (generally, whenever the wavelength is small compared to all other relevant length scales), light propagating in waveguides is only consistently described by wave optics, since the diameter of optical fibres is comparable to the optical wavelength. As a consequence, polarisation-dependent effects are not necessarily as strongly suppressed as in geometrical optics, where the transport equation for the polarisation arises only at second order in the
1/\lambda
-expansion, see e.g. the general discussion in Ref.
[
17
, Chapter III]
.

Further motivation for such investigations is provided by recent analyses of the perturbation of fibre-modes by Earth’s gravitational field
[
18
]
and by Earth’s rotation
[
19
]
. These calculations assume time-independent metrics, and the study of the influence of
gw
’s illustrates how optical fibres react to time-dependent perturbations of the space-time metric.

Of course, there are still many technical challenges still to be overcome before fibre-optic interferometers could be used in experimental gravitational wave detection, see e.g. the discussion in Ref.
[
20
, Appendix B]
.
Apart from the limited power handling capacity of optical fibres due to intensity damage and the emergence of non-linear effects at high power levels, the main obstructions seem to be shot noise (which is much more pronounced than in free-space interferometers due to fibre attenuation, cf. Refs.
[
21
,
22
]
), as well as thermal and mechanical noises inside the fibre (both of which are absent in free-space interferometers).
As these noise sources are more pronounced for long interferometer arms, we expect the detection of
gw
’s in fibre-optic interferometers to be viable only for signals of higher frequencies than those measured in
ligo
and Virgo (which are in the frequency range of roughly
30\,\text{to}\,250\,\mathrm{Hz}
, see e.g. Refs.
[
23
,
24
,
25
,
26
]
).
Consequently, it is to be expected that fibre-optic interferometers would be most suitable for searching for continuous signals (recent searches for continuous
gw
’s in
ligo
data can be found e.g. in Refs.
[
27
,
28
,
29
]
).
Moreover, since such interferometers can be made much smaller than the hitherto constructed free-space interferometers, it is conceivable to construct fibre-optic interferometers of multiple arms, providing more detailed position information on the
gw
source than the standard two-arm Michelson interferometers.
We plan to assess the experimental feasibility of such fibre-optic interferometers in the near future.

To summarise our findings: we consider a gravitational wave in transverse-traceless gauge of the form

(1.1)

propagating in an arbitrary direction
{\hat{\kappa}}
. (Here, we restrict the discussion to monochromatic plane waves. For arbitrary waveforms, one may use the Fourier transform and apply the analysis provided here to every Fourier component separately.)
Within this
gw
background, we solve perturbatively the Maxwell equations for (single-mode) step-index optical fibres and obtain the following result.
Choosing the
z
-axis to be the symmetry axis of the waveguide, we find the perturbed optical phase for fibres much shorter than the
gw
wavelength (
\omega_{g}z\ll 1
) to be

\psi=\omega t-\beta z+m\theta-\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon c_{1}\mathchoice{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}{z}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}{z}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}{z}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}{z}}}}\,\omega z\cos(\omega_{g}t)\,,

(1.2)

where
\omega
is the angular frequency of the mode,
\beta
the propagation constant and

the azimuthal mode index.

(1.3)

where
\varDelta=(n_{1}-n_{2})/n_{1}
is the relative difference of the refractive indices in the core (
n_{1}
) and the cladding (
n_{2}
).
The coefficients
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
arising here are computed numerically for typical single-mode fibres.
We find that
c_{1}\approx\bar{n}
, where
\bar{n}
is the (frequency dependent) effective refractive index of the fibre, and
c_{2}\approx 0.3
.
The diagonal terms (of opposite sign) describe birefringence, while the off-diagonal terms (of equal sign) describe a deformation of linear polarisation to elliptic polarisation.

Figure 1:
Dependence of three terms
\mathchoice{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}{z}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}{z}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}{z}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}{z}}}}
,

and
\mathchoice{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{y}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{y}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{y}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{y}}}}
(in absolute value) on the angle
\vartheta
formed by the optical and the gravitational wave-vectors.
The blue curve (“phase”) describes the angular dependence of the overall phase shift, the dashed orange line (“birefringence”) describes the difference in phase shift between the two polarisation states of light, and the dotted red curve (“deformation”) quantifies the mixing of the polarisation states.

Figure

1
shows how these three effects (phase shift, birefringence and polarisation deformation) depend on the angle
\vartheta
between the direction of light propagation and the direction of the gravitational wave. The precise dependence on this angle and on the polarisation of the gravitational wave is discussed in detail in
Section

8
.

It is seen that the phase shift is maximal when the gravitational wave propagates orthogonally to the fibre axis, and vanishes when it propagates collinearly with the light ray (i.e. parallel or antiparallel). For collinear propagation, the polarisation-dependent effects are maximal: the birefringence effect is then sensitive to the
+
polarisation of the gravitational wave, while the deformation effect is sensitive to the
\times
polarisation.

le$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}=0}
), so we are using
tt
coordinates.

We wish to describe the propagation of light in a cylindrical waveguide at rest in this coordinate system.
More precisely, we consider a cylindrical step-index fibre consisting of a linear dielectric with constant refractive index
n_{1}
in the core and
n_{2}
in the cladding (with
n_{1}>n_{2}
). Such waveguides are typically non-magnetic (i.e. of permeability
\upmu=1
) and hence the permittivity is
\upepsilon=\sqrt{n}
.

We will neglect boundary effects from the ends of the waveguide and the outer boundary of the cladding (the waveguide is thus modelled as infinitely long and the cladding as infinitely thick), and we shall neglect deformations of the fibre, so that in cylindrical coordinates
r,\theta,z
(related to the above
tt
coordinates as in flat space) the core-cladding interface is located at
r=\rho
.
A more accurate model would have to take into account the perturbation of the spatial metric when formulating the boundary conditions, but at such a level of accuracy we estimate that one would also have to consider elastic deformations of the waveguide due to the gravitational wave, which is beyond the scope of this article.

Being concerned with
weak
gravitational waves of
low
frequency, we work in a perturbative setting with two expansion parameters

\displaystyle\varepsilon

\displaystyle\ll 1\,,

\displaystyle\Omega

\displaystyle:=\omega_{g}/\omega\ll 1\,,

(2.2)

where
\varepsilon
is the
gw
amplitude,
\omega_{g}
its frequency, and
\omega
is the laser frequency.

3
Decomposition of the Maxwell Equations

In this section, we write the Maxwell equations in the
gw
background (
2.1
) in a 3+1 form, following Ref.
[
18
]
with
c=1
.
To this end, set

(3.2)

where
\epsilon
is the spatial volume form (not to be confused with the gravitational wave amplitude
\varepsilon
).
Here and henceforward, we neglect terms of second and higher order in
\varepsilon
and, for brevity, we will not write the error term
O(\varepsilon^{2})
explicitly.
The field equations then take the standard form

(3.7)

As the spatial metric is perturbed by the gravitational wave, this is the only part where the field equations differ from those of flat space.

To further simplify the equations, we define the Riemann-Silberstein vector

\mathchoice{Z^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}:=\upmu\mathchoice{D^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{D^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{D^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{D^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}+jn\mathchoice{B^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{B^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{B^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c] \displaystyle n\mathchoice{\dot{Z}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\dot{Z}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\dot{Z}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\dot{Z}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}+j\mathchoice{\epsilon^{{{i}{j}{k}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\epsilon^{{{i}{j}{k}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\epsilon^{{{i}{j}{k}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\epsilon^{{{i}{j}{k}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{j}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{j}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{j}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{j}}}}(\mathchoice{\mathrm{g}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{k}{l}}}}{\mathrm{g}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{k}{l}}}}{\mathrm{g}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{k}{l}}}}{\mathrm{g}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{k}{l}}}}\mathchoice{Z^{{{l}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{l}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{l}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{l}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}})=0\,,

(3.9)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}\mathchoice{Z^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=0\,,

(3.10)

wherever
n
is locally constant.

In the following calculations, we will use
two
independent (commuting) imaginary units
i
and
j
, where
i
is reserved for the usual complex description of waves (i.e. the physical fields are the
i
-real parts) and
j
is used for this compact formulation of the field equations.

As a side remark: the algebraic structure here is that of the complex vector space
\mathbf{C}^{2}
with
1
identified with
(1,0)
and
j
identified with
(0,1)
, and the product
(a,b)\times(c,d):=(ac-bd,ad+bc)
, where
a,b,c,d
are usual complex numbers. In particular, the product
ij=ji
is represented by
(i,0)\times(0,1)=(0,i)
, which is not equal to
-1
, here identified with
(-1,0)
.

Using only a single complex unit, it would not be possible to use this complex description of waves while simultaneously encoding the entire electromagnetic field in a single vector field. Indeed, if
D
and
B
are complex, then a field of the form
Z^{\prime}=\upmu D+inB
would only provide incomplete information about
D
and
B
, as it mixes the real part of
D
with the imaginary part of
B
, and vice versa. To recover those two fields entirely, one would have to use a second complex field
Z^{\prime\prime}=\upmu D-inB
. In contrast, the field
Z
defined in (
3.8
) allows obtaining the
i
-complex fields
D
and
B
simply by separating the
j
-real and
j
-imaginary parts.

4
The Unperturbed Modes

In this section, we briefly review the unperturbed solution to the problem in a notation which is convenient for the following calculations.

4.1
Adapted Basis

To describe the modes in a cylindrical waveguide, it is useful to use the complex frame

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}_{\parallel}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathfrak{e}_{z}\,,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}_{+}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\mathfrak{e}_{r}-i\mathfrak{e}_{\theta})\,,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}_{-}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\mathfrak{e}_{r}+i\mathfrak{e}_{\theta})\,,

(4.1)

where
(\mathfrak{e}_{r},\mathfrak{e}_{\theta},\mathfrak{e}_{z})
is the standard orthonormal frame adapted to cylindrical coordinates
(r,\theta,z)
.
The associated coframe is given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}^{\parallel}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}z\,,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}^{+}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\mathrm{d}r+ir\mathrm{d}\theta)\,,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}^{-}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\mathrm{d}r-ir\mathrm{d}\theta)\,.

(4.2)

The metric of flat space can then be written as

\delta=\mathfrak{e}^{+}\otimes\mathfrak{e}^{-}+\mathfrak{e}^{-}\otimes\mathfrak{e}^{+}+\mathfrak{e}^{\parallel}\otimes\mathfrak{e}^{\parallel}\,,

(4.3)

so raising and lowering of indices interchanges
+
and
-
, e.g. for a vector
v
one has
{\mathchoice{v^{{{\pm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{v^{{{\pm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{v^{{{\pm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{v^{{{\pm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=\mathchoice{v^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mp}}}}{v^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mp}}}}{v^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mp}}}}{v^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mp}}}}}
but
\mathchoice{v^{{{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{v^{{{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{v^{{{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{v^{{{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=\mathchoice{v^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{v^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{v^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{v^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}
.
The only non-vanishing components of the spatial Levi-Civita connection one-form are

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\omega^{{{+}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{+}}}}{\omega^{{{+}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{+}}}}{\omega^{{{+}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{+}}}}{\omega^{{{+}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{+}}}}

\displaystyle=+i\mathrm{d}\theta=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}r}\left(\mathfrak{e}^{+}-\mathfrak{e}^{-}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\omega^{{{-}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.63333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.16666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.63333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.16666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{-}}}}{\omega^{{{-}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.63333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.16666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.63333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.16666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{-}}}}{\omega^{{{-}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.63333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.16666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.63333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.16666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{-}}}}{\omega^{{{-}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.63333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.16666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.63333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.16666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{-}}}}

\displaystyle=-i\mathrm{d}\theta=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}r}\left(\mathfrak{e}^{-}-\mathfrak{e}^{+}\right)\,.

(4.4)

We find the cross-products to be

\mathfrak{e}_{\parallel}\times\mathfrak{e}_{\pm}=\pm i\mathfrak{e}_{\pm}\,,\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathfrak{e}_{+}\times\mathfrak{e}_{-}=i\mathfrak{e}_{\parallel}\,,

(4.5)

and consequently

\mathfrak{e}_{+}\cdot(\mathfrak{e}_{-}\times\mathfrak{e}_{\parallel})=+i\,,

(4.6)

so that for the Levi-Civita tensor (the volume form) we obtain

\mathchoice{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.63333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.16666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{+}{-}{\parallel}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.63333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.16666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{+}{-}{\parallel}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.63333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.16666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{+}{-}{\parallel}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.63333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.16666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{+}{-}{\parallel}}}}:=\epsilon(\mathfrak{e}_{+},\mathfrak{e}_{-},\mathfrak{e}_{\parallel})=+i\,,\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathchoice{\epsilon^{{{+}{-}{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.63333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.16666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\epsilon^{{{+}{-}{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.63333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.16666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\epsilon^{{{+}{-}{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.63333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.16666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\epsilon^{{{+}{-}{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.69446pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.63333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.16666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=-i\,.

(4.7)

4.2
Unperturbed Bessel Modes

The computation of the electromagnetic modes in a cylindrical step-index waveguide is a standard calculation which is discussed in many textbooks, e.g. Refs.
[
33
,
34
,
35
]
.
Here, we merely state the result, expressed in terms of the
j
-complex field
Z
and referred to the
i
-complex basis introduced above.

The unperturbed Bessel modes take the concise form

\displaystyle\mathchoice{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}

\displaystyle=f_{0}(\alpha;r)e^{i(\omega t-\beta z+m\theta)}\,,

(4.8)

\displaystyle i\zeta\mathchoice{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\pm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\pm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\pm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\pm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\rho^{2}(\beta\pm ijn\omega)c_{m}^{\pm}\mathchoice{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\,,

(4.9)

where

\displaystyle\zeta

\displaystyle=\rho^{2}(n^{2}\omega^{2}-\beta^{2})\,,

(4.10)

and

\displaystyle c_{m}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\partial_{r}\mp\tfrac{m}{r}\,.

(4.11)

Here,
\omega
is the angular frequency of the wave,
\beta
the propagation constant,
m
is the azimuthal mode index, and
\rho
is the radius of the fibre core. The radial function
f_{0}
is given by

f_{0}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2};r)=\begin{cases}\alpha_{1}J_{m}(Ur/\rho)\,,&r<\rho\,,\\
\alpha_{2}K_{m}(Wr/\rho)\,,&r>\rho\,,\end{cases}

(4.12)

where
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}
are
j
-complex constants (related by appropriate continuity conditions discussed below), and

U=\sqrt{\rho^{2}(n_{1}^{2}\omega^{2}-\beta^{2})}\,,\qquad\text{and}\qquad W=\sqrt{\rho^{2}(\beta^{2}-n_{2}^{2}\omega^{2})}\,.

(4.13)

The function
f_{0}
satisfies the Bessel equation

\mathrm{B}_{0}f_{0}(\alpha;r)\equiv\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial r^{2}}+\frac{1}{r}\frac{\partial}{\partial r}-\frac{m^{2}}{r^{2}}+\frac{\zeta}{\rho^{2}}\right]f_{0}(\alpha;r)=0\,,

(4.14)

both in the core and the cladding of the fibre.

In passing, let us note that the
c_{m}^{\pm}
act as “ladder operators”, i.e. for any real number
\lambda
one has the identities

c_{m}^{\pm}J_{m}(\lambda r)=\mp\lambda J_{m\pm 1}(\lambda r)\,,\qquad\text{and}\qquad c_{m}^{\pm}K_{m}(\lambda r)=-\lambda K_{m\pm 1}(\lambda r)\,,

(4.15)

see e.g. Ref.
[
36
, Eq. 9.1.27 on p. 361 and Eq. 9.6.26 on p. 376]
, so that we may write the
\pm
-components of the field as

i\zeta\mathchoice{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\pm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\pm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\pm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\pm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\rho(\beta\pm ijn\omega)f_{\pm}(\alpha;r)e^{i(\omega t-\beta z+m\theta)}\,,

(4.16)

where

f_{\pm}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2};r)=\rho c_{m}^{\pm}f_{0}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2};r)=\begin{cases}\mp\alpha_{1}UJ_{m\pm 1}(Ur/\rho)\,,&r<\rho\,,\\
-\alpha_{2}WK_{m\pm 1}(Wr/\rho)\,,&r>\rho\,.\end{cases}

(4.17)

The dispersion relation is then obtained by imposing continuity conditions at the core-cladding interface.
As we will discuss these conditions extensively for the perturbed problem, we merely outline the basic reasoning here.

1.

Maxwell’s equations imply that the field components
\mathchoice{D^{{{r}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{D^{{{r}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{D^{{{r}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{D^{{{r}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}},\mathchoice{E^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.30026pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64305pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\theta}}}}{E^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.30026pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64305pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\theta}}}}{E^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.30026pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64305pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\theta}}}}{E^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.30026pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64305pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\theta}}}},\mathchoice{E^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}}}}{E^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}}}}{E^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}}}}{E^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}}}},\mathchoice{B^{{{r}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{B^{{{r}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{B^{{{r}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{B^{{{r}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.92825pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.34679pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.67627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}},\mathchoice{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.30026pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64305pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\theta}}}}{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.30026pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64305pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\theta}}}}{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.30026pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64305pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\theta}}}}{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.809pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.30026pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64305pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\theta}}}},\mathchoice{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}}}}{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}}}}{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}}}}{H^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}}}}
are continuous at the core-cladding interface (at
r=\rho
).

2.

Only four of these six continuity conditions are linearly independent.

3.

Since the fields have two
j
-complex, and thus four
j
-real parameters, there is a
j
-real equation of the form
\text{(jumps)}=\Pi_{0}\text{(parameters)}
, where
\Pi_{0}
is a
4\times 4
matrix.

4.

The continuity conditions are thus only solvable if
\det\Pi_{0}=0
, which constitutes the dispersion relation for
\beta
and
\omega
. This equation is transcendental (as it contains various Bessel functions) and must thus be solved numerically.

5
The Perturbed Modes

As we shall see in this section, the effect of weak
gw
’s on light in waveguides is twofold:
(i) the phase is modulated by the
gw
, and
(ii) due to the tensorial nature of the
gw
amplitude, optical sidebands with an angular dependence of the form
e^{i(m+k)\theta}
, with
k\neq 0
, arise.
We find the dominant effects to be (a) the correction of the phase (
k=0
) and (b) the perturbation of the polarisation, which arises when
m+k=-m
.

Outline

Let us first give an outline of the calculation.
We start by deriving and solving the first order correction to the wave equation for the longitudinal field component.
From this, we derive the transverse components of the electromagnetic field, finding structures similar to the ones of the unperturbed case.
Schematically, the correction to the central mode can be written as

Z^{(1)}_{0}=[f^{+}(\alpha)+g^{+}(\sigma^{+})]e^{i(\omega t-\beta z+m\theta+\kappa.x)}+[f^{-}(\alpha)+g^{-}(\sigma^{-})]e^{i(\omega t-\beta z+m\theta-\kappa.x)}\,,

(5.1)

where the coefficients
\alpha
describe the unperturbed field (they act as source terms in the wave equation for
Z^{(1)}
) and the coefficients
\sigma^{\pm}
parametrise homogeneous solutions of the wave equation.
The continuity condition then takes the schematic form

\Pi_{0}\alpha+\varepsilon[{\mathrm{P}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}+\Sigma^{+}\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{+}]e^{+i\kappa.x}+\varepsilon[{\mathrm{P}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}+\Sigma^{-}\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{-}]e^{-i\kappa.x}=0\,,

(5.2)

where
\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\pm}
are column vectors, each comprising four
j
-real numbers which parametrise
\alpha
and
\sigma^{\pm}
, and
\Pi_{0},{\mathrm{P}},\Sigma^{\pm}
are
j
-real
4\times 4
-matrices.

The first term is the same as in the unperturbed problem, so the dispersion relation
\det\Pi_{0}=0
and the coefficients
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
remain unchanged.
The coefficients
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\pm}
are then determined from

\Sigma^{\pm}\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\pm}+{\mathrm{P}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}=0\,.

(5.3)

For the central mode, we find that
\Sigma^{\pm}
is close to the singular matrix
\Pi_{0}
and its inverse has the form
(\Sigma^{\pm})^{-1}=\pm\Omega^{-1}\Sigma^{\prime}+O(\Omega^{0})
, so that the parameters
\boldsymbol{\sigma}
are given by

\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\pm}=\mp\Omega^{-1}\Sigma^{\prime}{\mathrm{P}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}+O(\Omega^{0})\,.

(5.4)

In
Section

6
, we show that this correction of order
1/\Omega
amounts to a perturbation to the phase of the electromagnetic wave.

For the sidebands, we obtain similar equations. In almost all cases (
m+k\neq-m
), the coefficient matrices are
not
close to being singular, and hence no terms of order
1/\Omega
arise there.
However, in the exceptional case
m+k=-m
we have
\det\Pi_{k}=\det\Pi_{0}=0
, so that we again obtain terms of order
1/\Omega
there. We show in
Section

7
that these terms describe the perturbation of the polarisation of the electromagnetic wave.

The Polarisation of the Gravitational Wave

We start by writing the polarisation tensor
A
of the
gw
, as defined in (
2.1
), in the complex frame introduced above.
Using

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[\mathfrak{e}^{+}e^{+i\theta}+\mathfrak{e}^{-}e^{-i\theta}]\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\tfrac{-i}{\sqrt{2}}[\mathfrak{e}^{+}e^{+i\theta}-\mathfrak{e}^{-}e^{-i\theta}]\,,

(5.5)

one finds the components of

A=\mathchoice{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}\otimes\mathrm{d}x^{\nu}=\mathchoice{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}\mathfrak{e}^{i}\otimes\mathfrak{e}^{j}

(5.6)

in the basis
\mathfrak{e}_{+},\mathfrak{e}_{-},\mathfrak{e}_{\parallel}
to be

\displaystyle A_{\parallel\parallel}

\displaystyle=A_{zz}\,,

(5.7)

\displaystyle A_{+-}

\displaystyle=A_{-+}=\tfrac{1}{2}(A_{xx}+A_{yy})\,,

(5.8)

\displaystyle A_{\pm\parallel}

\displaystyle=A_{\parallel\pm}=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(A_{xz}\mp iA_{yz})e^{\pm i\theta}\,,

(5.9)

\displaystyle A_{++}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}(A_{xx}-A_{yy}-2iA_{xy})e^{+2i\theta}\,,

(5.10)

\displaystyle A_{--}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}(A_{xx}-A_{yy}+2iA_{xy})e^{-2i\theta}\,.

(5.11)

The condition that
A
is traceless now translates to

2A_{+-}+A_{\parallel\parallel}=0\,,

(5.12)

Appendix

A

is constant, the field equations (
3.9
) and (

(5.16)

where
\Delta
is the
perturbed
spatial vector Laplacian (i.e. associated to the perturbed spatial metric
\mathrm{g}
) and
\mathchoice{R^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{R^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{R^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}{R^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}}
is the spatial Ricci tensor.
As the exterior derivative is independent of the connection (provided it is torsion-free), it suffices to use the unperturbed Levi-Civita connection here:
\nabla\equiv\nabla^{{(0)}}
.

Let us now expand the electromagnetic field
Z=Z^{(0)}+\varepsilon Z^{(1)}
as well as the Laplacian
{\Delta=\Delta^{(0)}+\varepsilon\Delta^{(1)}}
.
Since
Z^{(0)}
satisfies the unperturbed equation
n^{2}\ddot{Z}^{(0)}-\Delta^{(0)}Z^{(0)}=0
, only terms of order
\varepsilon
remain, so we obtain at first order

(5.17)

where we have set

\square\mathchoice{Z^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}:=n^{2}\mathchoice{\ddot{Z}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\ddot{Z}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\ddot{Z}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\ddot{Z}^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}-(\Delta^{(0)}Z)^{i}\,.

(5.18)

The derivatives of the unperturbed field are of order
\omega
, which is much larger than the terms arising from derivatives of the metric or the spatial Ricci tensor, so we neglect the last two terms in (
5.17
), which are suppressed by
\Omega=\omega_{g}/\omega
or more.
At this level of accuracy, we also have

(5.19)

With these approximations, we arrive at the wave equation

(5.20)

We shall use one further natural approximation:

\cos(\kappa.x)=\cos(\omega_{g}(t-\mathchoice{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.10768pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.49423pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.7816pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{z}}}}z-r(\mathchoice{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}}}}\cos\theta+\mathchoice{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{y}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebo (5.21)

which is admissible since the diameter of the waveguide is negligible compared to the
gw
wavelength (
\rho\omega_{g}\ll 1
).
As an illustration: for typical optical fibres,
\rho
measures a couple of micrometers and
gw
wavelengths detected by
ligo
measure hundreds of kilometres:
\rho\omega_{g}\sim 10^{-10}
, so the error terms here will be much larger than those arising from quadratic terms in the
gw
amplitude
\varepsilon\sim 10^{-21}
.
At this level of accuracy, we may thus replace
\kappa.x
by
\kappa.x_{\parallel}:=\omega_{g}(t-\mathchoice{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}z)
to obtain

(5.22)

Using the fact that the connection one-form satisfies

(5.23)

Note that the unperturbed field
Z^{(0)}
depends on the angle
\theta
only as
e^{im\theta}
.
Since
A
has components which vary with
\theta
as
e^{\pm i\theta}
and
e^{\pm 2i\theta}
, the first order correction will have sidebands with
m
shifted by one and two in either direction.
Moreover, we find it convenient to separate terms which oscillate like the metric perturbation (i.e. trigonometric functions of
\kappa.x_{\parallel}
) into terms with complex exponentials and thus decompose the perturbation of the electromagnetic field (and other quantities if needed) as

\mathchoice{Z^{{{{(1)}}{a}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{{(1)}}{a}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{{(1)}}{a}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{{(1)}}{a}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=\sum_{k=-2}^{2}\sum_{l=\pm 1}\mathchoice{{\mathcal{Z}}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}{l}}}}{{\mathcal{Z}}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}{l}}}}{{\mathcal{Z}}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}{l}}}}{{\mathcal{Z}}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt t-\beta z+(m+k)\theta+l\,\kappa.x_{\parallel})}\,.

(5.24)

Thus, in
\mathchoice{{\mathcal{Z}}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}{l}}}}{{\mathcal{Z}}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}{l}}}}{{\mathcal{Z}}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}{l}}}}{{\mathcal{Z}}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][ the first index refers to the vector component (typically taken with respect to the complex frame

), the first lower index determines the angular mode number, and the second lower index determines the sign in the complex exponential

For the wave equation, we discuss the central mode (
k=0
) and the sidebands (
k\neq 0
) separately.

, so we find

(\mathchoice{A^{{{j}{k}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{A^{{{j}{k}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{A^{{{j}{k}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{A^{{{j}{k}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{k}\mathchoice{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616 (5.28)

and for the sidebands with
m
shifted by
\pm 2
, the relevant terms come from
\mathchoice{A^{{{\mp}{\mp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{A^{{{\mp}{\mp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{A^{{{\mp}{\mp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{A^{{{\mp}{\mp}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=\mathchoice{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\pm}{\pm}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makeb , so
