(5.73)

where the
\mathchoice{{\mathcal{Z}}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}{l}}}}{{\mathcal{Z}}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}{l}}}}{{\mathcal{Z}}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}{l}}}}{{\mathcal{Z}}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}{l}}}}
are the components from the decomposition (
5.24
), and where

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\chi^{{{\parallel}}}_{{{\phantom{\pm}}{0}}}}{\chi^{{{\parallel}}}_{{{\phantom{\pm}}{0}}}}{\chi^{{{\parallel}}}_{{{\phantom{\pm}}{0}}}}{\chi^{{{\parallel}}}_{{{\phantom{\pm}}{0}}}}

\displaystyle=a_{0}\mathchoice{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\chi^{{{\parallel}}}_{{{\pm 1}}}}{\chi^{{{\parallel}}}_{{{\pm 1}}}}{\chi^{{{\parallel}}}_{{{\pm 1}}}}{\chi^{{{\parallel}}}_{{{\pm 1}}}}

\displaystyle=a_{\pm 1}\mathchoice{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\pm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\pm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\pm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{{(0)}}{\pm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\chi^{{{\parallel}}}_{{{\pm 2}}}}{\chi^{{{\parallel}}}_{{{\pm 2}}}}{\chi^{{{\parallel}}}_{{{\pm 2}}}}{\chi^{{{\parallel}}}_{{{\pm 2}}}}

\displaystyle=0\,.

(5.74)

Similar to the decomposition (
5.24
), we write

\left\llbracket{Z}\right\rrbracket\equiv\left(\left\llbracket{Z}\right\rrbracket^{(0)}+\varepsilon\sum_{k=-2}^{+2}\sum_{l=\pm}\left\llbracket{Z}\right\rrbracket^{(1)}_{kl}e^{i(k\theta+l\kappa.x_{\parallel})}\right)e^{i(\omega t-\beta z+m\theta)}=0\,,

(5.75)

where the first term does not contain any
\varepsilon
corrections because all components of
Z^{(1)}
oscillate with
\exp(\pm i\kappa.x_{\parallel})
.

The four
j
-real components of
\left\llbracket{Z}\right\rrbracket^{(1)}_{kl}
are parametrised by
\alpha
(coming from the particular solutions) and
\sigma_{kl}
(homogeneous solutions), each having four
j
-real parameters. Hence, one can write

\left\llbracket{Z}\right\rrbracket_{kl}^{(1)}=a_{k}(M_{kl}\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}+\Sigma_{kl}\,\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{kl})\qquad\text{(no summation implied)}\,,

(5.76)

where
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
and
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{kl}
are
j
-real vectors:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\alpha}

\displaystyle=(\Re\alpha_{1},\Re\alpha_{2},\Im\alpha_{1},\Im\alpha_{2})^{\mathsf{T}}\,,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{kl}

\displaystyle\text{ similar},

(5.77)

and
M_{kl}
and
\Sigma_{kl}
are
j
-real
4\times 4
matrices. Note that the jumps are
not
simply linear in the complex coefficients
\alpha,\sigma_{kl}
since (
5.67
) discriminates between the real and complex parts.

In
Section

B.1
, it is shown that because the electric and magnetic fields are derived from a single complex field, these matrices are fully determined by their first two rows, which motivates the following abbreviating notation:

\begin{vmatrix}a&b&c&d\\
e&f&g&h\end{vmatrix}:=\begin{pmatrix}a&b&c&d\\
e&f&g&h\\
-c/n_{1}&-d/n_{2}&a/n_{1}&b/n_{2}\\
-g\;n_{1}&-h\;n_{2}&e\;n_{1}&f\;n_{2}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(5.78)

As many matrices considered here have similar structures, we define the following abbreviation

\begin{bmatrix}a_{1}&a_{2}&a_{3}&a_{4}\\
\beta&\omega&U&W\end{bmatrix}_{\nu}:=\\
\begin{vmatrix}-ia_{1}\rho\beta J_{\nu}^{\prime}(U)&-ia_{2}\rho\beta K_{\nu}^{\prime}(W)&a_{3}n_{1}\nu\rho\omega J_{\nu}(U)&a_{4}n_{2}\nu\rho\omega K_{\nu}(W)\\
\frac{1}{n_{1}^{2}}J_{\nu}(U)&-\frac{1}{n_{2}^{2}}K_{\nu}(W)&0&0\end{vmatrix}\,,

(5.79)

where the parameters
a_{1},\ldots,a_{4}
and
\beta,\omega
determine the numerical coefficients in the first row of the matrix, and the last two parameters
U,W
determine the arguments of the Bessel functions, whose order is given by the integer
\nu
which is given as a general subscript.

Within this class of matrices, we identify the following families, which are useful for the problem considered here:

\displaystyle\Pi_{k}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}1/U&1/W&1/U^{2}&1/W^{2}\\
\beta&\omega&U&W\end{bmatrix}_{m+k}\,,

(5.80)

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\pm}_{k}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}1/U_{\pm}&1/W_{\pm}&1/U_{\pm}^{2}&1/W_{\pm}^{2}\\
\beta_{\pm}&\omega_{\pm}&U_{\pm}&W_{\pm}\end{bmatrix}_{m+k}\,,

(5.81)

where
\beta_{\pm},\omega_{\pm}
are defined in (
5.33
) and (
5.34
), and
U_{\pm},W_{\pm}
are defined in (
5.36
).
The
\Pi
family is used to describe the central mode and to formulate the dispersion relation, and the
\Sigma
matrices arise when considering the jumps of the homogeneous solutions to the radial equations.

Similar to the family (
5.79
), we define

\begin{Bmatrix}a_{1}&a_{2}&a_{3}&a_{4}\\
\beta&\omega&U&W\end{Bmatrix}_{\nu}:=\\
\begin{vmatrix}-ia_{1}\rho\beta Y_{\nu}^{\prime}(U)&-ia_{2}\rho\beta I_{\nu}^{\prime}(W)&a_{3}n_{1}\nu\rho\omega Y_{\nu}(U)&a_{4}n_{2}\nu\rho\omega I_{\nu}(W)\\
\frac{1}{n_{1}^{2}}Y_{\nu}(U)&-\frac{1}{n_{2}^{2}}I_{\nu}(W)&0&0\end{vmatrix}\,,

(5.82)

where the Bessel functions
J_{\nu}
and
K_{\nu}
are replaced by
Y_{\nu}
and
I_{\nu}
.
Using this notation, let

\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}}^{\pm}_{k}

\displaystyle=\begin{Bmatrix}U/U_{\pm}^{2}&W/W_{\pm}^{2}&1/U_{\pm}^{2}&1/W_{\pm}^{2}\\
\beta_{\pm}&\omega_{\pm}&U&W\end{Bmatrix}_{m+k}\Lambda_{m+k}^{\pm}\,,

(5.83)

where

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\nu}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\diag\left(\Gamma^{\pm}_{\nu}(1),\Delta^{\pm}_{\nu}(1),\Gamma^{\pm}_{\nu}(1),\Delta^{\pm}_{\nu}(1)\right)\,.

(5.84)

This is used to describe the jumps of the particular solutions
p_{\nu}^{\pm}
defined in (
5.39
).
The functions
\Gamma^{\pm}_{\nu},\Delta^{\pm}_{\nu}
appearing here are defined in (
5.40
) and (
5.42
), while the functions
\tilde{\Gamma}^{\pm}_{\nu},\tilde{\Delta}^{\pm}_{\nu}
defined in (
5.41
) and (
5.43
) do not contribute, since they vanish at the core-cladding interface
r=\rho
.

Having prepared the notation, we now analyse (
5.75
). By linear independence of the exponential functions, all terms
\left\llbracket{Z}\right\rrbracket^{(0)}
and
\left\llbracket{Z}\right\rrbracket^{(1)}_{kl}
must vanish separately.

Dispersion Relation

Because all corrections coming from the gravitational wave oscillate as
\exp(\pm i\kappa.x_{\parallel})
, the first term in (
5.75
) is the same as for the unperturbed problem.
As shown explicitly in
Section

B.2
, the continuity condition for this part is given by

\Pi_{0}\boldsymbol{\alpha}=0\,,

(5.85)

where
\Pi_{0}
is given by (
5.80
).
To obtain a non-trivial solution, the coefficient matrix must be singular:

\det\Pi_{0}=0\,,

(5.86)

which is equivalent to

\left(\mathscr{J}_{m}+\mathscr{K}_{m}\right)\left(n_{1}^{2}\mathscr{J}_{m}+n_{2}^{2}\mathscr{K}_{m}\right)=\left(m\beta/\omega\right)^{2}\left(U^{-2}+W^{-2}\right)^{2}\,,

(5.87)

where we have used the abbreviations

\displaystyle\mathscr{J}_{m}

\displaystyle=\frac{J_{m}^{\prime}(U)}{UJ_{m}(U)}\,,

\displaystyle\mathscr{K}_{m}

\displaystyle=\frac{K_{m}^{\prime}(W)}{WK_{m}(W)}\,,

(5.88)

cf. e.g. Ref.
[
34
, Eq. 3.27 on p. 124]
and Ref.
[
35
, Eq. 16.166 on p. 511]
(where
\epsilon_{2}/\epsilon_{2}
should read
\epsilon_{1}/\epsilon_{2}
).
Because
\beta
and
\omega
enter this equation through
U
and
W
as arguments of Bessel functions, this is a transcendental equation which must be solved numerically. More precisely, by solving
\det\Pi_{0}=0
for a prescribed vacuum wavelength
\lambda=2\pi/\omega
and given waveguide parameters (i.e. core radius
\rho
and refractive indices
n_{1},n_{2}
), one can determine the effective refractive index
\bar{n}:=\beta/\omega
as a function of
\lambda
:
\bar{n}=\bar{n}(\lambda)
, which constitutes the waveguide dispersion relation. Subsequently, the coefficients
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
can be determined from (
5.85
) up to an overall factor which describes the amplitude of the electromagnetic wave.

In general, there are multiple solutions to this equation for given waveguide parameters and given values of
m
and
\omega
.
Here, we focus on
single-mode
fibres (i.e. fibres with sufficiently thin cores, operated at suitable frequencies) where there is only one single solution for
m=+1
and there are no solutions for higher values of
m
.

Central Mode

For the central mode (
k=0
), the continuity condition can be written as

\Sigma_{0}^{\pm}\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{0,\pm}+{\mathrm{P}}^{\pm}_{0}\Xi\boldsymbol{\alpha}+\tfrac{1}{2}\Psi_{0}\boldsymbol{\alpha}=0\,.

(5.89)

The first term describes the jumps arising from the homogeneous solution in (
5.45
).
In the second term, which comes from the particular solution, the matrix
\Xi
describes the discontinuity of the factor
\tfrac{1}{4}(\zeta-2\rho^{2}\beta^{2})
in (
5.45
):

\Xi=-\tfrac{1}{4}\diag(2\rho^{2}\beta^{2}-U^{2},2\rho^{2}\beta^{2}+W^{2},2\rho^{2}\beta^{2}-U^{2},2\rho^{2}\beta^{2}+W^{2})\,.

(5.90)

Finally, the last term contains all contributions from the
\chi
-terms in (
5.65
), (
5.66
) and (
5.73
):

\Psi_{0}=\begin{Bmatrix}\tfrac{1}{2}U_{3}^{2}/U^{3}&\tfrac{1}{2}W_{3}^{2}/W^{3}&U_{2}^{2}/U^{4}&W_{2}^{2}/W^{4}\\
\beta&\omega&U&W\end{Bmatrix}_{m}\,,

(5.91)

where

\displaystyle U_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=+\rho^{2}(n_{1}^{2}\omega^{2}-2\beta^{2})\,,

\displaystyle U_{3}^{2}

\displaystyle=+\rho^{2}(n_{1}^{2}\omega^{2}-3\beta^{2})\,,

(5.92)

\displaystyle W_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\rho^{2}(n_{2}^{2}\omega^{2}-2\beta^{2})\,,

\displaystyle W_{3}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\rho^{2}(n_{2}^{2}\omega^{2}-3\beta^{2})\,.

(5.93)

At the considered level of accuracy, we may neglect terms of order
\Omega
in
{\mathrm{P}}^{\pm}_{0}
.
This matrix contains the functions
\Gamma^{\pm}_{\nu}
and
\Delta^{\pm}_{\nu}
(via the matrix
\Lambda_{\pm}
). In (
5.40
) and (
5.42
) we may thus replace
U_{\pm}
and
W_{\pm}
by
U
and
W
which yields the integrals

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\nu}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi}{2}\int_{0}^{z}J_{\nu}(Uz^{\prime})^{2}z^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}\,,

(5.94)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\nu}(z)

\displaystyle=-\int_{z}^{\infty}K_{\nu}(Wz^{\prime})^{2}z^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}\,,

(5.95)

which also arise in similar calculations describing rotating waveguides. They evaluate to

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\nu}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{z^{2}}{2}\left(J_{\nu}(Uz)^{2}-J_{\nu-1}(Uz)J_{\nu+1}(Uz)\right)\,,

(5.96)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\nu}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{z^{2}}{2}\left(K_{\nu}(Wz)^{2}-K_{\nu-1}(Wz)K_{\nu+1}(Wz)\right)\,.

(5.97)

Expanding
\Sigma_{0,\pm}=\Pi_{0}\pm\Omega\Sigma^{(1)}+O(\Omega^{2})
, we obtain the inverse

\displaystyle(\Sigma_{0}^{\pm})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\pm\Omega^{-1}\Sigma^{\prime}+O(\Omega^{0})\,,

(5.98)

where

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\trace(\Sigma^{(1)}\adjugate\Pi_{0})}\adjugate\Pi_{0}\,.

(5.99)

Here,
\adjugate\Pi_{0}
denotes the adjugate of
\Pi_{0}
, which is the transpose of the cofactor matrix.
This leads to

\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{0,\pm}=\mp\Omega^{-1}\Sigma^{\prime}({\mathrm{P}}_{0}\Xi+\tfrac{1}{2}\Psi)\boldsymbol{\alpha}+O(\Omega)\,.

(5.100)

Let us analyse this result in a bit more detail.
First,
\Sigma^{\prime}
can be re-expressed in a more transparent way by considering the denominator
\trace(\Sigma^{(1)}\adjugate\Pi_{0})=\partial_{\omega_{g}}\det\Sigma_{0}^{+}
. Since
\Sigma_{0}^{+}
is obtained from
\Pi_{0}
by the substitution
\omega\to\omega+\omega_{g}
and
\beta\to\beta+\mathchoice{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}\omega_{g}
, one has

\trace(\Sigma^{(1)}\adjugate\Pi_{0})=\partial_{\omega}\det\Pi_{0}+\mathchoice{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}\partial_{\beta}\det\Pi_{0}\,.

(5.101)

The ratio of these partial derivatives has a physical interpretation: differentiating the defining equation for the dispersion relation
\det\Pi_{0}(\beta(\omega),\omega)=0
with respect to
\omega
yields

\beta^{\prime}\equiv\frac{\partial\beta}{\partial\omega}=-\frac{\partial_{\omega}\det\Pi_{0}}{\partial_{\beta}\det\Pi_{0}}\,,

(5.102)

so the ratio describes how the propagation constant
\beta
changes with varying frequency
\omega
(i.e. the reciprocal group velocity).
Equivalently, this can be expressed in terms of the effective refractive index
\bar{n}=\bar{n}(\omega)
as

\beta^{\prime}=\bar{n}+\omega\frac{\partial\bar{n}}{\partial\omega}\,.

(5.103)

Hence, one obtains the alternative formula

\Sigma^{\prime}=-\frac{\partial_{\beta}\det\Pi_{0}}{\beta^{\prime}-\mathchoice{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}}\adjugate\Pi_{0}\,.

(5.104)

Next, using the definition of the adjugate and the fact that
\Pi_{0}
is singular, we have
\Pi_{0}\Sigma^{\prime}=0
, so
\operatorname{im}\Sigma^{\prime}\subset\operatorname{ker}\Pi_{0}
. But since the kernel of
\Pi_{0}
is one-dimensional and spanned by
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
, it is seen that
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{0,\pm}
is again proportional to
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
.
This leads to the general formula

\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{0,\pm}=\mp\frac{1}{4\Omega}\frac{c_{1}}{\beta^{\prime}-\mathchoice{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}\,,

(5.105)

where
c_{1}
is a coefficient depending on the waveguide parameters, which we compute numerically.
The overall factor
\mp 1/4
was chosen in analogy to similar results in vacuum, where
c_{1}=\beta^{\prime}=1
.
This equation essentially determines the phase induced by the gravitational wave, as is shown in detail in
Section

6
.
There, we also show that appropriate emission conditions ensure that no phase shift arises if the
gw
is collinear with the symmetry axis of the waveguide.

Sidebands

For the sidebands, we find the continuity conditions to be of the form

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\pm}_{+1}C_{+}\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{+1,\pm}-\tfrac{i}{\sqrt{2}}{\mathrm{P}}^{\pm}_{+1}C_{+1}\boldsymbol{\alpha}+\tfrac{1}{2}\Psi_{+1}\boldsymbol{\alpha}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(5.106)

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\pm}_{-1}C_{-}\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{-1,\pm}-\tfrac{i}{\sqrt{2}}{\mathrm{P}}^{\pm}_{-1}C_{-1}\boldsymbol{\alpha}+\tfrac{1}{2}\Psi_{-1}\boldsymbol{\alpha}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(5.107)

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\pm}_{+2}(C_{+})^{2}\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{+2,\pm}+\tfrac{1}{4}{\mathrm{P}}^{\pm}_{+2}C_{+2}\boldsymbol{\alpha}+\tfrac{1}{2}\Psi_{+2}\boldsymbol{\alpha}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(5.108)

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\pm}_{-2}(C_{-})^{2}\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{-2,\pm}+\tfrac{1}{4}{\mathrm{P}}^{\pm}_{-2}C_{-2}\boldsymbol{\alpha}+\tfrac{1}{2}\Psi_{-2}\boldsymbol{\alpha}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(5.109)

where the matrices
C_{k}
account for the coefficients
\mp U
and
-W
in (
4.15
)

\displaystyle C_{\pm 1}

\displaystyle=\diag\left(\mp U,-W,\mp U,-W,\right)\,,

\displaystyle C_{\pm 2}

\displaystyle=\diag\left(U^{2},W^{2},U^{2},W^{2}\right)\,,

(5.110)

and the
\Psi
-matrices are given by

\displaystyle\Psi_{\pm 1}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(2\rho\beta\,\Psi_{\pm 1}^{(a)}+\Psi_{\pm 1}^{(b)}\right)\,,

(5.111)

where

\displaystyle\Psi_{\pm 1}^{(a)}

\displaystyle=\begin{vmatrix}\pm\frac{\rho\beta}{U^{2}}J_{m\pm 1}^{\prime}&-\frac{\rho\beta}{W^{2}}K_{m\pm 1}^{\prime}&\pm in_{1}\frac{m\pm 1}{U}\frac{\rho\omega}{U^{2}}J_{m\pm 1}&-in_{2}\frac{m\pm 1}{W}\frac{\rho\omega}{W^{2}}K_{m\pm 1}\\
0&0&0&0\end{vmatrix}\,,

(5.112)

\displaystyle\Psi_{\pm 1}^{(b)}

\displaystyle=\begin{vmatrix}-\frac{m\pm 1}{U}J_{m\pm 1}&\mp\frac{m\pm 1}{W}K_{m\pm 1}&0&0\\
\pm i\frac{\rho\beta}{n_{1}^{2}U}J_{m\pm 1}&+i\frac{\rho\beta}{n_{2}^{2}W}K_{m\pm 1}&+\frac{\rho\omega}{n_{1}U}J_{m\pm 1}&\pm\frac{\rho\omega}{n_{2}W}K_{m\pm 1}\end{vmatrix}\,,

(5.113)

and

\displaystyle\Psi_{\pm 2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\begin{vmatrix}\mp i\frac{\rho\beta}{U}J_{m\pm 1}&-i\frac{\rho\beta}{W}K_{m\pm 1}&0&0\\
0&0&0&0\end{vmatrix}\,.

(5.114)

Here, the arguments of the Bessel functions have been suppressed for brevity:
J_{\nu}=J_{\nu}(U)
and
K_{\nu}=K_{\nu}(W)
.

Since we are concerned with single-mode fibres, barring one exception which is discussed below, all coefficient matrices
\Sigma^{\pm}_{k}
with
k\neq 0
are invertible. This is because for vanishing
\Omega
they reduce to the non-singular matrices
\Pi_{k}
(with
k\neq 0
), and invertibility is maintained for sufficiently small
\Omega
by continuity.
Thus, as a first approximation one can replace
\Sigma^{\pm}_{k}
by
\Pi_{k}
to obtain

\displaystyle\text{for }k=\pm 1:\qquad\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{k,\pm}

\displaystyle=-C_{k}^{-1}\Pi_{k}^{-1}\left(\tfrac{1}{2}\Psi_{k}-\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}\rho\beta{\mathrm{P}}_{k}C_{k}\right)\boldsymbol{\alpha}+O(\Omega)\,,

(5.115)

\displaystyle\text{for }k=\pm 2:\qquad\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{k,\pm}

\displaystyle=-C_{k}^{-1}\Pi_{k}^{-1}\left(\tfrac{1}{2}\Psi_{k}+\tfrac{1}{4}{\mathrm{P}}_{k}C_{k}\right)\boldsymbol{\alpha}+O(\Omega)\,,

(5.116)

which shows explicitly that the sideband amplitudes are of order
\varepsilon
only, and thus much smaller than the corrections of the central mode of order
\varepsilon\omega/\omega_{g}
.

The exception to the above case is the following.
Since

\displaystyle J_{-\nu}(z)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\nu}J_{\nu}(z)\,,

\displaystyle K_{-\nu}(z)

\displaystyle=+K_{\nu}(z)\,,

(5.117)

see e.g. Ref.
[
36
, eqns. 9.1.5 on p. 358 and 9.6.6 on p. 375]
,
the dispersion relation (
5.87
) is invariant under
m\to-m
,
which implies that if
m+k=-m
, i.e.
k=-2m
, then
\Pi_{k}
is singular again.
In our case, since
k
ranges from
-2
to
+2
, this arises only if
m=\pm 1
, so this effect
does
occur in single-mode fibres.
Here, we restrict the discussion to the case
m=+1
, as the alternative
m=-1
is completely analogous.
For this special case
m=+1
and
k=-2
, the matrices
\Sigma^{\pm}_{-2}
are close to being singular:

\Sigma^{\pm}_{-2}=\Pi_{-2}\pm\Omega\,\delta\Sigma_{-2}+O(\Omega^{2})\,,

(5.118)

which has the inverse

\displaystyle(\Sigma^{\pm}_{-2})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\pm\Omega^{-1}\Sigma^{\prime\prime}+O(\Omega^{0})\,,

(5.119)

where

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\trace[(\delta\Sigma_{-2})\adjugate(\Pi_{-2})]}\adjugate(\Pi_{-2})\,,

(5.120)

so that instead of (
5.116
) we obtain for
k=-2

C_{-2}\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{-2,\pm}=\mp\Omega^{-1}\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\left(\tfrac{1}{2}\Psi_{-2}+\tfrac{1}{4}P_{-2}C_{-2}\right)\boldsymbol{\alpha}+O(\Omega^{0})\,.

(5.121)

Similar to
\Pi_{0}
whose kernel is spanned by
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
, the kernel of
\Pi_{-2}
is also one-dimensional and spanned by

\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{*}=(+\alpha_{1},-\alpha_{2},-\alpha_{3},+\alpha_{4})\,.

(5.122)

Since the range of
\Sigma^{\prime\prime}
is the kernel of
\Pi_{-2}
, the two vectors
C_{-2}\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{-2,\pm}
are proportional to
\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{*}
, and we find, similar to the central mode

C_{-2}\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{-2,\pm}=\pm a_{-2}\frac{\varDelta^{2}}{2\Omega}\frac{c_{2}}{\beta^{\prime}-\mathchoice{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{*}\,,

(5.123)

where
\beta^{\prime}
is the same constant as in the phase correction (
5.105
), and the coefficient
c_{2}
is a new parameter, which we determine numerically.
Here, the relative index difference
\varDelta
is defined as

\varDelta=1-\frac{n_{2}}{n_{1}}\,,

(5.124)

and we have factored out
\varDelta^{2}/2
such that
c_{2}
is typically of order unity (see the numerical examples below).

As shown explicitly in
Section

7
, this formula essentially describes the perturbation of the polarisation by the gravitational wave.

6
Perturbation of the Phase

In this section, we implement boundary conditions which model a setup where monochromatic laser light of definite polarisation is injected into the fibre.
As we will see, contrary to the unperturbed case, this will require adding further terms to the field computed so far.
This leads to an expression for the phase correction which vanishes at the point where the light enters the fibre and grows with increasing distance. A similar construction for the eikonal in a non-dispersive regime can be found in Ref.
[
4
, Sect. 2]
.

Neglecting boundary effects from the ends of the waveguide, the light sent into the waveguide (or coming out of it) is simply obtained by restricting the field to a definite value of
z
, where we consider
z=0
for simplicity.
Also, we restrict the discussion to the “large corrections”, i.e. to
k=0
and
k=-2m
where the perturbations were found to be of order
1/\Omega
. The remaining “small components” are expected to be experimentally irrelevant.

Recall that the coefficients of the central mode (
k=0
) were given by

\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{0,\pm}=\mp\frac{1}{4\Omega}\frac{c_{1}}{\beta^{\prime}-\mathchoice{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}\,.

(6.1)

Since this is proportional to the unperturbed coefficients
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
, we may write the central mode (
k=0
) of the
overall
field in the form

\displaystyle\mathchoice{Z^{{{{(0)}}{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{0}}}}{Z^{{{{(0)}}{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{0}}}}{Z^{{{{(0)}}{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{0}}}}{Z^{{{{(0)}}{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{0}}}}+\varepsilon\mathchoice{Z^{{{{(1)}}{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{0}}}}{Z^{{{{(1)}}{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{0}}}}{Z^{{{{(1)}}{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{0}}}}{Z^{{{{(1)}}{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.23616pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.2611pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.47223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{0}}}}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{a}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{a}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{a}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{a}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\tilde{\Phi}\,,

(6.2)

where

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}

\displaystyle=f_{0}(\alpha;r)\,,

(6.3)

\displaystyle i\zeta\mathchoice{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{\pm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{\pm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{\pm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{\pm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.25002pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\rho(\beta\pm ijn\omega)f_{\pm}(\alpha;r)\,,

(6.4)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Phi}

\displaystyle=e^{i\psi^{(0)}}\left[1-\frac{\varepsilon}{4\Omega}\frac{a_{0}c_{1}}{\beta^{\prime}-\mathchoice{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}}\left(e^{+i\kappa.x_{\parallel}}-e^{-i\kappa.x_{\parallel}}\right)\right]\,,

(6.5)

\displaystyle\psi^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\omega t-\beta z+m\theta\,.

(6.6)

Evaluating the perturbation terms at
z=0
, we find that this describes a situation where the incoming phase is modulated in a highly specific way by the gravitational wave. This is not the situation which we want to model so that correction terms are needed: instead of the phase modulation behaviour given by
\tilde{\Phi}
, we are concerned with the scenario where a polarised monochromatic plane wave (as emitted by a laser source) is injected into the fibre at
z=0
.
Hence, we require the injected field to have a definite phase of
\psi=\omega t+m\theta
(and definite polarisation, see the next section) at
z=0
.

Such boundary conditions can be implemented by adding appropriate solutions of the unperturbed equations of slightly shifted frequencies.
Specifically, we add terms of the form

\mathchoice{\mathfrak{z}^{{{a}}}_{{{\pm}}}}{\mathfrak{z}^{{{a}}}_{{{\pm}}}}{\mathfrak{z}^{{{a}}}_{{{\pm}}}}{\mathfrak{z}^{{{a}}}_{{{\pm}}}}=\mathchoice{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{a}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{a}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{a}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{a}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\exp(i(\omega_{\pm}t-(\beta\pm\omega_{g}\beta^{\prime})z+m\theta))\,,

(6.7)

where the term
\beta^{\prime}=\partial\beta/\partial\omega
ensures that the unperturbed dispersion relation is satisfied.
(The shift in the frequency also causes a slight perturbation in the amplitude, but this is of order
\Omega
and thus negligible here.)
The corrected overall field is thus

\mathchoice{Z^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{0}}}}{Z^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{0}}}}{Z^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{0}}}}{Z^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{0}}}}=\mathchoice{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{a}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{a}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{a}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{a}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\tilde{\Phi}+\frac{\varepsilon}{4\Omega}\frac{a_{0}c_{1}}{\beta^{\prime}-\mathchoice{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}}(\mathchoice{\mathfrak{z}^{{{a}}}_{{{+}}}}{\mathfrak{z}^{{{a}}}_{{{+}}}}{\mathfrak{z}^{{{a}}}_{{{+}}}}{\mathfrak{z}^{{{a}}}_{{{+}}}}-\mathchoice{\mathfrak{z}^{{{a}}}_{{{-}}}}{\mathfrak{z}^{{{a}}}_{{{-}}}}{\mathfrak{z}^{{{a}}}_{{{-}}}}{\mathfrak{z}^{{{a}}}_{{{-}}}})=\mathchoice{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{a}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{a}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{a}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{Z}^{{{a}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\Phi\,,

(6.8)

where

\Phi=e^{i\psi^{(0)}}\left[1-\frac{\varepsilon}{4\Omega}\frac{a_{0}c_{1}}{\beta^{\prime}-\mathchoice{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}}\left(e^{+i\omega_{g}t}(e^{-i\omega_{g}\mathchoice{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68059pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68059pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68059pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68059pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68059pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68059pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68059pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68059pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}z}-e^{-i\omega_{g}\beta^{\prime}z})-e^{-i\omega_{g}t}(e^{+i\omega_{g}\mathchoice{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68059pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68059pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68059pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68059pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68059pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68059pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.68059pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.68059pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.25pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}z}-e^{+i\omega_{g}\beta^{\prime}z})\right)\right]\,.

(6.9)

Assuming the fibre to be much shorter than the
gw
wavelength, i.e.
\omega_{g}z\ll 1
, one finally obtains

\Phi\approx e^{i\psi^{(0)}}\left[1-\tfrac{i}{2}\varepsilon a_{0}c_{1}\cos(\omega_{g}t)\omega z\right]\approx e^{i\psi^{(0)}}\exp(-\tfrac{i}{2}\varepsilon a_{0}c_{1}\cos(\omega_{g}t)\omega z)\,,

(6.10)

which can be written as a perturbation of the phase:

\psi=\omega t-\beta z+m\theta-\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon a_{0}c_{1}\omega z\cos(\omega_{g}t)\,.

(6.11)

This shows that the phase perturbation grows linearly with the distance from the emission point and oscillates in time exactly as the gravitational wave does.

7
Perturbation of the Polarisation

Having identified the perturbation of the central mode
k=0
as a correction to the phase,
we now show that the other “large term” with
m+k=-m
corresponds to a perturbation of the polarisation.

To see this, we follow Ref.
[
34
]
, where it is shown that modes with real field patterns, i.e. an angular dependence of the form
\cos\theta
or
\sin\theta
, correspond to linear polarisation. To describe such fields, one must take linear combinations of the solutions with
m=+1
and
m=-1
, and we shall make the standard assumption of weak guidance
\varDelta\equiv(n_{1}-n_{2})/n_{1}\ll 1
.
It should be noted that non-zero values of
\varDelta
cause the modes not to be
perfectly
linearly polarised, but these deviations are independent of
t
and
z
and can thus not be mistaken for gravitational wave signals.

Let us briefly review the construction in the unperturbed case. For brevity, we restrict the discussion to the field in the core, as the analysis of the cladding is completely analogous.
Assuming weak guidance, the longitudinal field components are

\displaystyle\text{for }m=+1:\qquad\mathchoice{Z^{{{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=(1-ij)J_{+1}(Ur/\rho)e^{i(\omega t-\beta z+\theta)}\,,

(7.1)

\displaystyle\text{for }m=-1:\qquad\mathchoice{Z^{{{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{Z^{{{\parallel}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=(1+ij)J_{-1}(Ur/\rho)e^{i(\omega t-\beta z-\theta)}\,,

cf. Ref.
[
34
, Section 3.2]
.
The overall normalisation is arbitrary and of no concern for the discussion here.
At the level of the amplitude vector
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
, this means

\displaystyle\text{for }m=+1:\qquad\alpha_{1}+j\alpha_{2}

\displaystyle=1-ij\,,

(7.2)

\displaystyle\text{for }m=-1:\qquad\alpha^{*}_{1}+j\alpha^{*}_{2}

\displaystyle=1+ij\,.

Plugging this into the expressions for the unperturbed field (
4.9
), setting
\beta=n\omega
with
n\approx n_{1}
and computing the Cartesian components via (
5.5
), one obtains the so-called
HE
-modes

\text{HE}_{1,\pm 1}:\qquad\begin{pmatrix}Z^{x}&Z^{y}\end{pmatrix}=\mathscr{A}(r)\begin{pmatrix}\mp i-j&1\mp ij\end{pmatrix}\,,

(7.3)

where
\mathscr{A}(r)=\frac{n\rho\omega}{U}J_{0}(Ur/\rho)
. The second index distinguishes the cases
m=+1
and
m=-1
.
The so-called LP-modes are then given by the linear combinations

\displaystyle\text{LP}_{0,1,x}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{i}{2}(\text{HE}_{1,+1}-\text{HE}_{1,-1})\,,

(7.4)

\displaystyle\text{LP}_{0,1,y}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}(\text{HE}_{1,+1}+\text{HE}_{1,-1})\,,

whose
x
- and
y
-components are given by

\displaystyle\text{LP}_{0,1,x}:\qquad\begin{pmatrix}Z^{x}&Z^{y}\end{pmatrix}=\mathscr{A}(r)\begin{pmatrix}+1&+j\end{pmatrix}\,,

(7.5)

\displaystyle\text{LP}_{0,1,y}:\qquad\begin{pmatrix}Z^{x}&Z^{y}\end{pmatrix}=\mathscr{A}(r)\begin{pmatrix}-j&+1\end{pmatrix}\,.

These modes are thus linearly polarised in the
x
- and
y
-directions, respectively.

This construction can be carried over directly to the perturbed problem.
For this, we determine the coefficients of the “large sidebands” to be

\displaystyle\text{for }m=+1:\qquad C_{-2}\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{-2,\pm}

\displaystyle=\pm a_{-2}\frac{\varDelta^{2}}{2\Omega}\frac{c_{2}}{\beta^{\prime}-\mathchoice{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{*}\,,

(7.6)

\displaystyle\text{for }m=-1:\qquad C_{+2}\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{+2,\pm}

\displaystyle=\pm a_{+2}\frac{\varDelta^{2}}{2\Omega}\frac{c_{2}}{\beta^{\prime}-\mathchoice{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}\,.

(7.7)

As for the central mode, we impose boundary conditions such that these sidebands do not contribute at
z=0
.
We may thus repeat the argument from the previous section to find that these sidebands grow (for
\omega_{g}z\ll 1
) linearly with the distance
z
and oscillate in time as
\cos(\omega_{g}t)
, which is described by the function

\tilde{f}(t,z):=c_{2}\varDelta^{2}\cos(\omega_{g}t)\,\omega z\,.

(7.8)

With this, one finds (up to the overall phase correction from before) that the perturbation of the longitudinal component of the field is obtained from the unperturbed expression by the substitution

\displaystyle(1-ij)J_{+1}e^{+i\theta}

\displaystyle\mapsto(1-ij)J_{+1}e^{+i\theta}+i\varepsilon a_{-2}\tilde{f}(t,z)(1+ij)J_{+1}e^{-i\theta}\,,

(7.9)

\displaystyle(1+ij)J_{-1}e^{-i\theta}

\displaystyle\mapsto(1+ij)J_{-1}e^{+i\theta}+i\varepsilon a_{+2}\tilde{f}(t,z)(1+ij)J_{-1}e^{+i\theta}\,,

(7.10)

where the arguments of the Bessel functions have been suppressed for brevity: they are
{J_{\pm 1}=J_{\pm 1}(Ur/\rho)}
.
Using (
5.10
) and (
5.11
), the factor arising here evaluates to

ia_{\mp 2}=\tfrac{i}{2}(\mathchoice{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{x}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{x}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{x}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{x}}}}-\mathchoice{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{y}{y}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2 akebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{y}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{y}}}}\,.

(7.11)

Defining the abbreviations

\displaystyle\xi

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}(\mathchoice{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{x}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{x}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{x}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{x}}}}-\mathchoice{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{y}{y}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{y}{y}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{y}{y}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{y}{y}}}})\tilde{f}(t,z)\,,

\displaystyle\eta

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{y}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{y}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{y}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{y}}}}\tilde{f}(t,z)\,,

(7.12)

and forming the same linear combinations as in the unperturbed case, we find the
perturbed
LP-modes

\displaystyle\widetilde{\text{LP}}_{0,1,x}:\qquad\begin{pmatrix}Z^{x}&Z^{y}\end{pmatrix}=\mathscr{A}(r)\begin{pmatrix}+(1-i\varepsilon\xi)+ij\varepsilon\eta&+j(1-i\varepsilon\xi)-i\varepsilon\eta\end{pmatrix}\,,

(7.13)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\text{LP}}_{0,1,y}:\qquad\begin{pmatrix}Z^{x}&Z^{y}\end{pmatrix}=\mathscr{A}(r)\begin{pmatrix}-j(1+i\varepsilon\xi)-i\varepsilon\eta&+(1+i\varepsilon\xi)-ij\varepsilon\eta\end{pmatrix}\,.

Comparing with the unperturbed LP-modes (
7.5
), we find that the effect of the gravitational wave is the following transformation of the polarisation directions:

\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{e}_{x}\\
\mathfrak{e}_{y}\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}1-i\varepsilon\xi&-i\varepsilon\eta\\
-i\varepsilon\eta&1+i\varepsilon\xi\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{e}_{x}\\
\mathfrak{e}_{y}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(7.14)

The modes of different polarisation
\mathfrak{e}_{x}
and
\mathfrak{e}_{y}
thus obtain slightly different phase shifts coming from
1\mp i\varepsilon\xi\approx\exp(\mp i\varepsilon\xi)
, which arise whenever
\mathchoice{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{x}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{x}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{x}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{x}}}}\neq\mathchoice{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{y}{y}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{y}{y}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{y}{y}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{y}{y}}}}
. This can be interpreted as weak birefringence.

In contrast, the
\eta
-terms, which correspond to
\mathchoice{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{y}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{y}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{y}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{x}{y}}}}=\mathchoice{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{y}{x}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{y}{x}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{y}{x}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.30675pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.57817pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.84154pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.80048pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.00034pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{y}{x}}}}
, cannot be absorbed into the phase, as they describe weak deformations of linear polarisation into elliptic polarisation of alternating direction.

To obtain a definite interpretation in terms of polarisation states as measured by a
physical
polarisation filter or polarising beam splitter, one would have to model such devices in the perturbed metric. Accordingly, one might think that the above stated transformation could be absorbed in a redefinition of the polarisation directions. However, such redefinitions of the basis
cannot
alter the result significantly, as the perturbed basis is necessarily related to the unperturbed one by a
real
transformation, which cannot absorb the
complex
transformation coefficients found here. Hence, both the birefringence term
\xi
and the deformation term
\eta
are genuine physical effects and not merely coordinate artifacts.

For completeness, we also give the equivalent transformation in the complex basis
\mathfrak{f}_{\pm}=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\mathfrak{e}_{x}\pm i\mathfrak{e}_{y})
, which describes circular polarisation:

\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{f}_{+}\\
\mathfrak{f}_{-}\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}1&-\varepsilon(i\xi-\eta)\\
-\varepsilon(i\xi+\eta)&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{f}_{+}\\
\mathfrak{f}_{-}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(7.15)

Also in this basis, the effect of the gravitational wave is to introduce a weak mixing of the two polarisation states which grows with the distance from
z=0
and oscillates in time.

8
Numerical Examples and Angular Dependence

Having found the first order effect of weak gravitational waves on the guided modes, let us give some numerical examples.

Parameters of typical single-mode fibres at a vacuum wavelength
\lambda=1500\,\mathrm{nm}
.

Table

1
lists two sets of parameters for typical single-mode fibres (with
m=\pm 1
) operated at a vacuum wavelength of
\lambda=1500\,\mathrm{nm}
.
The first parameter set is the same as in Refs.
[
18
]
and
[
19
]
, where the influence of Earth’s gravitational field and its rotation on waveguides was analysed.
Both fibres have the same core radius and effective refractive index
\bar{n}
, but their constituents have different refractive indices
n_{1},n_{2}
. Hence, their values for the relative index difference
\varDelta=(n_{1}-n_{2})/n_{1}
and the numerical aperture
\mathrm{NA}=\sqrt{n_{1}^{2}-n_{2}^{2}}
also differ.

Table 2:
Numerical results for the coefficients
c_{1},c_{2}
and
\beta^{\prime}
.

We have implemented our calculations in
Wolfram Mathematica
and computed the quantities
c_{1},c_{2}
and
\beta^{\prime}
which arise in (
5.105
) and (
5.123
). The results are given in
Table

2
.
The inverse group velocity
\beta^{\prime}=\partial\beta/\partial\omega
is larger than the effective refractive index
\bar{n}
, which shows that the waveguide exhibits normal dispersion
\partial\bar{n}/\partial\omega>0\Leftrightarrow\partial\bar{n}/\partial\lambda<0
.

The “phase parameter”
c_{1}
determines the perturbation of the phase via (
6.11
).
As can be seen from
Table

1
, this quantity is comparable to the effective refractive index
\bar{n}
(or equally
n_{1}
or
n_{2}
): the relative differences are
0.12\%
and
0.14\%
, respectively, which is even smaller than the relative index difference
\varDelta
. For most applications, the approximation
c_{1}\approx\bar{n}
will thus be sufficient.

The “polarisation parameter”
c_{2}
determines the perturbation of the polarisation according to (
7.14
) and (
7.15
) via the function
\tilde{f}
defined in (
7.8
).
Here, we see the reason for factoring
\varDelta^{2}
in (
5.123
) and (
7.8
): for the two considered fibres, the values of
\varDelta^{2}
differ by a factor of approximately
1.6
, but the values of
c_{2}
are almost the same.

Together, the two parameters
c_{1},c_{2}
fully characterise the influence of the gravitational wave on the fibre modes derived here. To describe the angular dependence of these effects, we choose coordinates
x,y,z
where
z
is the distance along the symmetry axis of the waveguide and the wave-vector of the gravitational wave lies in the
xz
-plane.
The unit vector
\mathchoice{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.09723pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\parallel}}}}
in

and the
gw

\mathchoice{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}}}}
can then be parameterised by

\displaystyle(\mathchoice{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{{\hat{\kappa}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}})

\displaystyle=(\sin\vartheta,0,\cos\vartheta)\,,

(8.1)

\displaystyle(\mathchoice{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}}{A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.54285pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.98645pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.84746pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ij}}}})

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}h_{+}\cos^{2}\vartheta&h_{\times}\cos\vartheta&-h_{+}\cos\vartheta\sin\vartheta\\
h_{\times}\cos\vartheta&-h_{+}&-h_{\times}\sin\vartheta\\
-h_{+}\cos\vartheta\sin\vartheta&-h_{\times}\sin\vartheta&h_{+}\sin^{2}\vartheta\end{pmatrix}\,,

(8.2)

where
h_{+}
and
h_{\times}
are real constants describing the two possible polarisation states of the gravitational wave.
Here,
\vartheta
denotes the incidence angle of the gravitational wave, which is not to be confused with the angular coordinate of the cylindrical coordinate system
(r,\theta,z)
.

\displaystyle=h_{+}\sin^{2}\vartheta\,,
